And afterward I’m kind of shocked by how much it ended up being. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you help with, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure if I’d call it guilt every time. I mean, I don’t feel great about it afterward, but I also try not to beat myself up too much. It’s more like… embarrassment, maybe? Like, “Why did I do that again?” But I don’t know if that’s what you mean by guilty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but on those nights it does feel kind of like I’m not fully in charge. I’ll be telling myself, “Okay, put it away now,” but then I’m still opening another container or grabbing something else, and it just keeps going until everything’s gone. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for the amount you ate during those episodes by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. I’m kind of embarrassed to say that. It’s not like every single time, but after I get home and I’ve eaten way too much takeout or snacks, I’ll go to the bathroom and… make myself throw up. I tell myself it’s so it doesn’t just sit in me or turn into weight. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like, “Why did I do that again?” But I don’t know if that’s what you mean by guilty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but on those nights it does feel kind of like I’m not fully in charge. I’ll be telling myself, “Okay, put it away now,” but then I’m still opening another container or grabbing something else, and it just keeps going until everything’s gone. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for the amount you ate during those episodes by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. I’m kind of embarrassed to say that. It’s not like every single time, but after I get home and I’ve eaten way too much takeout or snacks, I’ll go to the bathroom and… make myself throw up. I tell myself it’s so it doesn’t just sit in me or turn into weight. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every night or anything. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But since the move kind of settled down and I got into a routine, I’d say most weeks there’s at least one late night where I eat way more than I meant to, and then I… try to make up for it somehow. Sometimes that’s throwing up, sometimes I’ll skip breakfast, or make myself go on a really long run. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and feel like you're not fully in charge, without then trying to make up for it by vomiting, exercising, or anything like that?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. Usually if it gets to that point, I end up doing something after, even if it’s just telling myself I’ll skip a meal or exercise more. There might be times where I just go to bed and feel awful about it, but I haven’t really kept track of those separately. I don’t know if that’s the kind of answer you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and feel like you're not fully in charge, without doing anything to try to make up for it afterward?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, there are some times where I don’t really do anything afterward. Not as often, I don’t think. But once I start on those nights, it’s like I’m sort of watching myself from the outside, telling myself to just put the food away, and my hands just keep opening containers until everything’s gone. Then I just feel awful and kind of stuck with it. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, um… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because my eating has been getting a little out of hand sometimes. After late shifts, I’ll stop and get food, and then when I get home I’ll just… keep eating. Like a full takeout meal, then chips, sweets, whatever’s around the apartment. And afterward I’m kind of shocked by how much it ended up being. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you help with, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure if I’d call it guilt every time. I mean, I don’t feel great about it afterward, but I also try not to beat myself up too much. It’s more like… embarrassment, maybe? Like, “Why did I do that again?” But I don’t know if that’s what you mean by guilty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but on those nights it does feel kind of like I’m not fully in charge. I’ll be telling myself, “Okay, put it away now,” but then I’m still opening another container or grabbing something else, and it just keeps going until everything’s gone. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for the amount you ate during those episodes by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. I’m kind of embarrassed to say that. It’s not like every single time, but after I get home and I’ve eaten way too much takeout or snacks, I’ll go to the bathroom and… make myself throw up. I tell myself it’s so it doesn’t just sit in me or turn into weight. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every night or anything. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But since the move kind of settled down and I got into a routine, I’d say most weeks there’s at least one late night where I eat way more than I meant to, and then I… try to make up for it somehow. Sometimes that’s throwing up, sometimes I’ll skip breakfast, or make myself go on a really long run. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and feel like you're not fully in charge, without then trying to make up for it by vomiting, exercising, or anything like that?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. Usually if it gets to that point, I end up doing something after, even if it’s just telling myself I’ll skip a meal or exercise more. There might be times where I just go to bed and feel awful about it, but I haven’t really kept track of those separately. I don’t know if that’s the kind of answer you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and feel like you're not fully in charge, without doing anything to try to make up for it afterward?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, there are some times where I don’t really do anything afterward. Not as often, I don’t think. Then I just feel awful and kind of stuck with it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes where you eat a lot and feel out of control without doing anything to make up for it afterward?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can give you a clean number. I haven’t really been separating those out in my head. I think it happens sometimes, but less than the times where I do try to “fix” it afterward. Maybe I should start keeping track, if that would be helpful?\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you have episodes where you eat a lot and feel out of control without doing anything to make up for it afterward?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know the exact number. I’m sorry—I know you’re asking for something more specific. I’d say it’s not a lot, maybe just a few times, but I haven’t been tracking which times I do something afterward and which times I don’t. If it helps, I can start keeping count.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes where you eat a lot and feel out of control without doing anything to make up for it happen at least once a week?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I don’t want to guess wrong. My sense is probably not every week, or at least I haven’t noticed it that regularly. It’s kind of blurry because I wasn’t separating those from the other times in my head. Is it okay if I say I’m not sure?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot and feeling out of control, without doing anything to make up for it, happen more than three times a month?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s more than three times a month… but I’m not completely confident. I feel a little bad that I haven’t been keeping track the right way. My best answer is probably no, not more than three, but I’d want to watch it more closely so I’m not misleading you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
